# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Store assistants manage a rented-car gallery for each store. Each store keeps a store name together with the car records, rental records, and overdue-notice records that belong to that location. Every car record stores a plate number, model, and daily price. Customer records store the renter's name, surname, and address. Rental records store the rental date, due date, return date, total price, and leasing terms, and connect the rented car with the customer who rents it. When a customer wants to rent a car, the system shows the cars that are currently available and sorts them by daily price. The system records each rental, calculates total rental revenue, lists overdue customers when the current date passes a due date and the car has not been returned, computes the average daily price of all cars in the store, and counts how many rentals each customer has made. The main office issues overdue notices for late returns, and every overdue notice keeps a notice number so the notice can be tracked for the target customer.
Function Requirement:
1. Identify the cars that are currently available in the store. A car is available when it is not tied to an active rental whose return date is still empty. Return the available cars in ascending order of daily price, or return an empty list when none are available.
2. Calculate the total revenue generated by all rental records stored in the store.
3. List the customers whose rentals are overdue. A rental is overdue when its return date is empty and the current date is later than the due date. Return an empty list when no overdue customers exist, or when the current date input is null.
4. Determine the average daily price of all cars stored in the store. Return 0.0 when the store has no car records.
5. Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the result as a customer-to-count mapping. Return an empty map when the store has no rental records for any customer.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title carrental Model

class Car {
    dailyPrice : double
    model : String
    plate : String
}
class Customer {
    address : String
    name : String
    surname : String
}
class OverdueNotice {
    noticeId : String
    customer : Customer
    sendNoticeTo(customer : Customer) : String
}
class Rental {
    backDate : EDate
    dueDate : EDate
    leasingTerms : String
    rentalDate : EDate
    totalPrice : double
    car : Car
    customer : Customer
}
class Store {
    name : String
    cars : List<Car>
    notices : List<OverdueNotice>
    rentals : List<Rental>
    calculateTotalRevenue() : double
    countCarsRentedPerCustomer() : EMap
    determineAverageDailyPrice() : double
    findCustomersWithOverdueRentals(currentDate : EDate) : List<Customer>
    identifyAvailableCars() : List<Car>
}

OverdueNotice --> Customer : customer
Rental --> Car : car
Rental --> Customer : customer
Store *-- "*" Car : cars
Store *-- "*" OverdueNotice : notices
Store *-- "*" Rental : rentals
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
OverdueNotice::sendNoticeTo
Summary: Records and tracks an overdue notice for a specified customer, returning a tracking identifier.
Algorithm: 
Related Features: noticeId, customer
Related Classes: Customer
Input: customer : Customer - The target customer who received the overdue notice.
Output: String - A tracking confirmation string containing the noticeId and customer reference.
Pre-condition: The OverdueNotice object is instantiated with a valid, non-null noticeId. The customer parameter is not null.
Post-condition: The notice is successfully linked to the provided customer. A non-null tracking string is returned. Internal state reflects the notice as issued/tracked.

# Output Format

class_1::operation_1

 * revised description for operation_1
 */

# Requirement
```text
Store assistants manage a rented-car gallery for each store. Each store keeps a store name together with the car records, rental records, and overdue-notice records that belong to that location. Every car record stores a plate number, model, and daily price. Customer records store the renter's name, surname, and address. Rental records store the rental date, due date, return date, total price, and leasing terms, and connect the rented car with the customer who rents it. When a customer wants to rent a car, the system shows the cars that are currently available and sorts them by daily price. The system records each rental, calculates total rental revenue, lists overdue customers when the current date passes a due date and the car has not been returned, computes the average daily price of all cars in the store, and counts how many rentals each customer has made. The main office issues overdue notices for late returns, and every overdue notice keeps a notice number so the notice can be tracked for the target customer.
Function Requirement:
1. Identify the cars that are currently available in the store. A car is available when it is not tied to an active rental whose return date is still empty. Return the available cars in ascending order of daily price, or return an empty list when none are available.
2. Calculate the total revenue generated by all rental records stored in the store.
3. List the customers whose rentals are overdue. A rental is overdue when its return date is empty and the current date is later than the due date. Return an empty list when no overdue customers exist, or when the current date input is null.
4. Determine the average daily price of all cars stored in the store. Return 0.0 when the store has no car records.
5. Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the result as a customer-to-count mapping. Return an empty map when the store has no rental records for any customer.
```

# Operation Specifications
Store::identifyAvailableCars
Summary: Filters and sorts cars that are not currently tied to an active (unreturned) rental, ordered by price.
Algorithm: 
Related Features: cars, rentals, Car.dailyPrice, Rental.car, Rental.backDate
Related Classes: Car, Rental
Input: None
Output: List<Car> - Available cars sorted by dailyPrice ascending. Empty list if all cars are rented or no cars exist.
Pre-condition: The Store instance exists. Both cars and rentals collections are initialized.
Post-condition: Returns a correctly filtered and sorted list of available vehicles. Original collections remain unmodified.

Store::findCustomersWithOverdueRentals
Summary: Identifies customers with active rentals that have passed their due date without being returned.
Algorithm: 
Related Features: rentals, Rental.backDate, Rental.dueDate, Rental.customer
Related Classes: Customer, Rental
Input: currentDate : EDate - The reference date used to evaluate overdue status.
Output: List<Customer> - A list of unique customers with overdue rentals. Empty if none qualify or if currentDate is null.
Pre-condition: The Store instance exists. currentDate can be null or a valid date.
Post-condition: Returns a list of distinct customers meeting the overdue criteria. Order is typically insertion-based. Store state remains unmodified.

Store::countCarsRentedPerCustomer
Summary: Generates a mapping of each customer to the number of rental records they have made.
Algorithm: 
Related Features: rentals, Rental.customer
Related Classes: Customer, Rental
Input: None
Output: EMap<Customer, Integer> - A mapping where keys are unique Customers and values are their respective rental counts. Empty if no rentals exist.
Pre-condition: The Store instance exists and the rentals collection is initialized.
Post-condition: Returns a complete mapping reflecting accurate rental counts per customer. If rentals is empty, returns an empty map. Store state is unmodified.

Store::calculateTotalRevenue
Summary: Computes the cumulative revenue generated from all rental records in the store.
Algorithm: 
Related Features: rentals, Rental.totalPrice
Related Classes: Rental
Input: None
Output: double - The sum of totalPrice values across all rental records. Non-negative.
Pre-condition: The Store instance exists and the rentals collection is initialized (may be empty).
Post-condition: Returns the exact arithmetic sum of all rental totalPrice fields. The store's internal state remains unchanged.

Store::determineAverageDailyPrice
Summary: Calculates the arithmetic mean of the daily prices of all cars in the store.
Algorithm: 
Related Features: cars, Car.dailyPrice
Related Classes: Car
Input: None
Output: double - The average daily price of all cars. >= 0.0.
Pre-condition: The Store instance exists and the cars collection is initialized.
Post-condition: Returns the precise average. If no cars exist, returns 0.0. No modifications to store state.

   * Summary: Filters and sorts cars that are not currently tied to an active (unreturned) rental, ordered by price.
   * Algorithm: 
   *   1. Initialize an empty list `availableCars`.
   *   2. For each `car` in `cars`:
   *      a. Set `isAvailable = true`.
   *      b. For each `rental` in `rentals`:
   *         i. If `rental.car` equals `car` AND `rental.backDate` is null:
   *            - Set `isAvailable = false` and break inner loop.
   *      c. If `isAvailable` is true, add `car` to `availableCars`.
   *   3. Sort `availableCars` in ascending order based on `dailyPrice`.
   *   4. Return `availableCars`.
   * Related Features: cars, rentals, Car.dailyPrice, Rental.car, Rental.backDate
   * Related Classes: Car, Rental
   * Input: None
   * Output: List<Car> - Available cars sorted by dailyPrice ascending. Empty list if no cars are available.
   * Pre-condition: The Store instance exists. Both cars and rentals collections are initialized.
   * Post-condition: Returns a correctly filtered and sorted list of available vehicles. Original collections remain unmodified.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public EList<Car> identifyAvailableCars()
  {
    EList<Car> availableCars = new BasicEList<Car>();
    for (Car car : getCars()) {
      boolean isAvailable = true;
      for (Rental rental : getRentals()) {
        if (rental.getCar() != null && rental.getCar().equals(car) && rental.getBackDate() == null) {
          isAvailable = false;
          break;
        }
      }
      if (isAvailable) {
        availableCars.add(car);
      }
    }
    availableCars.sort(Comparator.comparingDouble(Car::getDailyPrice));
    return availableCars;
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Computes the cumulative revenue generated from all rental records in the store.
   * Algorithm: 
   *   1. Initialize `totalRevenue = 0.0`.
   *   2. For each `rental` in `rentals`:
   *      a. `totalRevenue += rental.totalPrice`.
   *   3. Return `totalRevenue`.
   * Related Features: rentals, Rental.totalPrice

   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public double calculateTotalRevenue()
  {
    double totalRevenue = 0.0;
    for (Rental rental : getRentals()) {
      totalRevenue += rental.getTotalPrice();
    }
    return totalRevenue;
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Identifies customers with active rentals that have passed their due date without being returned.
   * Algorithm: 
   *   1. If `currentDate` is null, return an empty list.
   *   2. Initialize an empty set `overdueCustomers` to ensure uniqueness.
   *   3. For each `rental` in `rentals`:
   *      a. If `rental.backDate` is null AND `rental.dueDate` < `currentDate`:
   *         i. Add `rental.customer` to `overdueCustomers`.
   *   4. Convert `overdueCustomers` to a list and return it.
   * Related Features: rentals, Rental.backDate, Rental.dueDate, Rental.customer
   * Related Classes: Customer, Rental
   * Input: currentDate : EDate - The reference date used to evaluate overdue status.
   * Output: List<Customer> - A list of unique customers with overdue rentals. Empty if none qualify or if currentDate is null.
   * Pre-condition: The Store instance exists. currentDate can be null or a valid date.
   * Post-condition: Returns a list of distinct customers meeting the overdue criteria. Store state remains unmodified.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public EList<Customer> findCustomersWithOverdueRentals(final Date currentDate)
  {
    if (currentDate == null) {
      return new BasicEList<Customer>();
    }
    Set<Customer> overdueCustomers = new HashSet<Customer>();
    for (Rental rental : getRentals()) {
      if (rental.getBackDate() == null && rental.getDueDate() != null && rental.getDueDate().before(currentDate)) {
        Customer customer = rental.getCustomer();
        if (customer != null) {
          overdueCustomers.add(customer);
        }
      }
    }
    return new BasicEList<Customer>(overdueCustomers);
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Calculates the arithmetic mean of the daily prices of all cars in the store.
   * Algorithm: 
   *   1. If `cars` is empty, return 0.0.
   *   2. Initialize `priceSum = 0.0`.
   *   3. For each `car` in `cars`:
   *      a. `priceSum += car.dailyPrice`.
   *   4. Calculate `average = priceSum / cars.size()`.
   *   5. Return `average`.
   * Related Features: cars, Car.dailyPrice
   * Related Classes: Car
   * Input: None
   * Output: double - The average daily price of all cars. >= 0.0.
   * Pre-condition: The Store instance exists and the cars collection is initialized.
   * Post-condition: Returns the precise average. If no cars exist, returns 0.0. No modifications to store state.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public double determineAverageDailyPrice()
  {
    EList<Car> cars = getCars();
    if (cars.isEmpty()) {
      return 0.0;
    }
    double priceSum = 0.0;
    for (Car car : cars) {
      priceSum += car.getDailyPrice();
    }
    return priceSum / cars.size();
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Generates a mapping of each customer to the number of rental records they have made.
   * Algorithm: 
   *   1. Initialize an empty map `customerCountMap`.
   *   2. For each `rental` in `rentals`:
   *      a. Get `customer = rental.customer`.
   *      b. If `customer` is not in `customerCountMap`, initialize its count to 0.
   *      c. Increment count for `customer` in `customerCountMap` by 1.
   *   3. Return `customerCountMap`.
   * Related Features: rentals, Rental.customer
   * Related Classes: Customer, Rental
   * Input: None
   * Output: EMap<Customer, Integer> - A mapping where keys are unique Customers and values are their respective rental counts. Empty if no rentals exist.
   * Pre-condition: The Store instance exists and the rentals collection is initialized.
   * Post-condition: Returns a complete mapping reflecting accurate rental counts per customer. Store state is unmodified.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public Map<Customer, Integer> countCarsRentedPerCustomer()
  {
    Map<Customer, Integer> customerCountMap = new HashMap<Customer, Integer>();
    for (Rental rental : getRentals()) {
      Customer customer = rental.getCustomer();
      if (customer != null) {
        Integer count = customerCountMap.get(customer);
        if (count == null) {
          count = 0;
        }
        customerCountMap.put(customer, count + 1);
      }
    }
    return customerCountMap;
  }

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Store assistants manage a rented-car gallery for each store. Each store keeps a store name together with the car records, rental records, and overdue-notice records that belong to that location. Every car record stores a plate number, model, and daily price. Customer records store the renter's name, surname, and address. Rental records store the rental date, due date, return date, total price, and leasing terms, and connect the rented car with the customer who rents it. When a customer wants to rent a car, the system shows the cars that are currently available and sorts them by daily price. The system records each rental, calculates total rental revenue, lists overdue customers when the current date passes a due date and the car has not been returned, computes the average daily price of all cars in the store, and counts how many rentals each customer has made. The main office issues overdue notices for late returns, and every overdue notice keeps a notice number so the notice can be tracked for the target customer.
Function Requirement:
1. Identify the cars that are currently available in the store. A car is available when it is not tied to an active rental whose return date is still empty. Return the available cars in ascending order of daily price, or return an empty list when none are available.
2. Calculate the total revenue generated by all rental records stored in the store.
3. List the customers whose rentals are overdue. A rental is overdue when its return date is empty and the current date is later than the due date. Return an empty list when no overdue customers exist, or when the current date input is null.
4. Determine the average daily price of all cars stored in the store. Return 0.0 when the store has no car records.
5. Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the result as a customer-to-count mapping. Return an empty map when the store has no rental records for any customer.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title carrental Model

class Car {
    dailyPrice : double
    model : String
    plate : String
}
class Customer {
    address : String
    name : String
    surname : String
}
class OverdueNotice {
    noticeId : String
    customer : Customer
    sendNoticeTo(customer : Customer) : String
}
class Rental {
    backDate : EDate
    dueDate : EDate
    leasingTerms : String
    rentalDate : EDate
    totalPrice : double
    car : Car
    customer : Customer
}
class Store {
    name : String
    cars : List<Car>
    notices : List<OverdueNotice>
    rentals : List<Rental>
    calculateTotalRevenue() : double
    countCarsRentedPerCustomer() : EMap
    determineAverageDailyPrice() : double
    findCustomersWithOverdueRentals(currentDate : EDate) : List<Customer>
    identifyAvailableCars() : List<Car>
}

OverdueNotice --> Customer : customer
Rental --> Car : car
Rental --> Customer : customer
Store *-- "*" Car : cars
Store *-- "*" OverdueNotice : notices
Store *-- "*" Rental : rentals
@enduml
```

You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Store assistants manage a rented-car gallery for each store. Each store keeps a store name together with the car records, rental records, and overdue-notice records that belong to that location. Every car record stores a plate number, model, and daily price. Customer records store the renter's name, surname, and address. Rental records store the rental date, due date, return date, total price, and leasing terms, and connect the rented car with the customer who rents it. When a customer wants to rent a car, the system shows the cars that are currently available and sorts them by daily price. The system records each rental, calculates total rental revenue, lists overdue customers when the current date passes a due date and the car has not been returned, computes the average daily price of all cars in the store, and counts how many rentals each customer has made. The main office issues overdue notices for late returns, and every overdue notice keeps a notice number so the notice can be tracked for the target customer.
Function Requirement:
1. Identify the cars that are currently available in the store. A car is available when it is not tied to an active rental whose return date is still empty. Return the available cars in ascending order of daily price, or return an empty list when none are available.
2. Calculate the total revenue generated by all rental records stored in the store.
3. List the customers whose rentals are overdue. A rental is overdue when its return date is empty and the current date is later than the due date. Return an empty list when no overdue customers exist, or when the current date input is null.
4. Determine the average daily price of all cars stored in the store. Return 0.0 when the store has no car records.
5. Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the result as a customer-to-count mapping. Return an empty map when the store has no rental records for any customer.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title carrental Model

class Car {
    dailyPrice : double
    model : String
    plate : String
}
class Customer {
    address : String
    name : String
    surname : String
}
class OverdueNotice {
    noticeId : String
    customer : Customer
    sendNoticeTo(customer : Customer) : String
}
class Rental {
    backDate : EDate
    dueDate : EDate
    leasingTerms : String
    rentalDate : EDate
    totalPrice : double
    car : Car
    customer : Customer
}
class Store {
    name : String
    cars : List<Car>
    notices : List<OverdueNotice>
    rentals : List<Rental>
    calculateTotalRevenue() : double
    countCarsRentedPerCustomer() : EMap
    determineAverageDailyPrice() : double
    findCustomersWithOverdueRentals(currentDate : EDate) : List<Customer>
    identifyAvailableCars() : List<Car>
}

OverdueNotice --> Customer : customer
Rental --> Car : car
Rental --> Customer : customer
Store *-- "*" Car : cars
Store *-- "*" OverdueNotice : notices
Store *-- "*" Rental : rentals
@enduml
```

# Operations
+ OverdueNotice::sendNoticeTo
+ Store::calculateTotalRevenue
+ Store::countCarsRentedPerCustomer
+ Store::determineAverageDailyPrice
+ Store::findCustomersWithOverdueRentals
+ Store::identifyAvailableCars

# Operation Specifications
Store::identifyAvailableCars
Summary: Identifies and returns all cars currently available for rent, sorted by daily price in ascending order.
Algorithm: 
Related Features: cars, rentals, Rental.backDate, Rental.car, Car.dailyPrice
Related Classes: Car, Rental
Input: None
Output: List<Car> - Available cars sorted by daily price ascending. Returns an empty list when none are available.
Pre-condition: The `cars` and `rentals` lists are initialized and not null.
Post-condition: Returns a list of cars not associated with any active rental (null return date), strictly ordered by `dailyPrice` ascending. If no cars meet the criteria, returns an empty list.

Store::countCarsRentedPerCustomer
Summary: Counts the number of rental transactions per customer and returns the result as a key-value mapping.
Algorithm: 
Related Features: rentals, Rental.customer
Related Classes: Rental, Customer
Input: None
Output: EMap<Customer, Integer> - Mapping of customers to their respective rental counts. Returns an empty map when the store has no rental records.
Pre-condition: The `rentals` list is initialized.
Post-condition: Returns a map where keys are `Customer` objects and values represent the exact number of times each customer appears in `rentals`. Returns an empty map if `rentals` is empty.

Store::determineAverageDailyPrice
Summary: Calculates the arithmetic mean of the daily prices for all cars stored in the store.
Algorithm: 
Related Features: cars, Car.dailyPrice
Related Classes: Car
Input: None
Output: double - Average daily price. Returns 0.0 when the store has no car records.
Pre-condition: The `cars` list is initialized.
Post-condition: Returns the mean of `dailyPrice` values. If `cars` is empty, returns exactly 0.0. Division by zero is prevented by the initial check.

Store::calculateTotalRevenue
Summary: Computes and returns the aggregate revenue generated by all rental records in the store.
Algorithm: 
Related Features: rentals, Rental.totalPrice
Related Classes: Rental
Input: None
Output: double - Total calculated revenue. Returns 0.0 when no rental records exist.
Pre-condition: The `rentals` list is initialized.
Post-condition: Returns the exact arithmetic sum of `totalPrice` across all `Rental` objects. If the list is empty, returns exactly 0.0.

Store::findCustomersWithOverdueRentals
Summary: Retrieves a list of unique customers whose rentals are overdue based on a provided reference date.
Algorithm: 
Related Features: rentals, Rental.backDate, Rental.dueDate, Rental.customer
Related Classes: Rental, Customer
Input: currentDate : EDate - Reference date used to determine overdue status. Format: Standard date object. May be null.
Output: List<Customer> - Unique customers with overdue rentals. Returns an empty list when no overdue customers exist or when `currentDate` is null.
Pre-condition: The `rentals` list is initialized. `currentDate` may be null.
Post-condition: Returns a list of distinct `Customer` objects linked to rentals where the return date is unrecorded and the due date strictly precedes `currentDate`. Returns an empty list if `currentDate` is null or no matching rentals are found.

# Requirement
```text
Store assistants manage a rented-car gallery for each store. Each store keeps a store name together with the car records, rental records, and overdue-notice records that belong to that location. Every car record stores a plate number, model, and daily price. Customer records store the renter's name, surname, and address. Rental records store the rental date, due date, return date, total price, and leasing terms, and connect the rented car with the customer who rents it. When a customer wants to rent a car, the system shows the cars that are currently available and sorts them by daily price. The system records each rental, calculates total rental revenue, lists overdue customers when the current date passes a due date and the car has not been returned, computes the average daily price of all cars in the store, and counts how many rentals each customer has made. The main office issues overdue notices for late returns, and every overdue notice keeps a notice number so the notice can be tracked for the target customer.
Function Requirement:
1. Identify the cars that are currently available in the store. A car is available when it is not tied to an active rental whose return date is still empty. Return the available cars in ascending order of daily price, or return an empty list when none are available.
2. Calculate the total revenue generated by all rental records stored in the store.
3. List the customers whose rentals are overdue. A rental is overdue when its return date is empty and the current date is later than the due date. Return an empty list when no overdue customers exist, or when the current date input is null.
4. Determine the average daily price of all cars stored in the store. Return 0.0 when the store has no car records.
5. Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the result as a customer-to-count mapping. Return an empty map when the store has no rental records for any customer.
```

  public EList<Car> identifyAvailableCars()
  {

    for (Car car : getCars()) {
      boolean isRentedOut = false;
      for (Rental rental : getRentals()) {
        if (rental.getCar() == car && rental.getBackDate() == null) {
          isRentedOut = true;
          break;
        }
      }
      if (!isRentedOut) {
        available.add(car);
      }
    }
    available.sort((c1, c2) -> Double.compare(c1.getDailyPrice(), c2.getDailyPrice()));
    return available;
  }

  public EList<Customer> findCustomersWithOverdueRentals(final Date currentDate)
  {

    Set<Customer> uniqueCustomers = new HashSet<>();
    for (Rental rental : getRentals()) {
      if (rental.getBackDate() == null && rental.getDueDate().before(currentDate)) {
        uniqueCustomers.add(rental.getCustomer());
      }
    }
    return new BasicEList<>(uniqueCustomers);

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Store assistants manage a rented-car gallery for each store. Each store keeps a store name together with the car records, rental records, and overdue-notice records that belong to that location. Every car record stores a plate number, model, and daily price. Customer records store the renter's name, surname, and address. Rental records store the rental date, due date, return date, total price, and leasing terms, and connect the rented car with the customer who rents it. When a customer wants to rent a car, the system shows the cars that are currently available and sorts them by daily price. The system records each rental, calculates total rental revenue, lists overdue customers when the current date passes a due date and the car has not been returned, computes the average daily price of all cars in the store, and counts how many rentals each customer has made. The main office issues overdue notices for late returns, and every overdue notice keeps a notice number so the notice can be tracked for the target customer.
Function Requirement:
1. Identify the cars that are currently available in the store. A car is available when it is not tied to an active rental whose return date is still empty. Return the available cars in ascending order of daily price, or return an empty list when none are available.
2. Calculate the total revenue generated by all rental records stored in the store.
3. List the customers whose rentals are overdue. A rental is overdue when its return date is empty and the current date is later than the due date. Return an empty list when no overdue customers exist, or when the current date input is null.
4. Determine the average daily price of all cars stored in the store. Return 0.0 when the store has no car records.
5. Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the result as a customer-to-count mapping. Return an empty map when the store has no rental records for any customer.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title carrental Model

class Car {

You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Store assistants manage a rented-car gallery for each store. Each store keeps a store name together with the car records, rental records, and overdue-notice records that belong to that location. Every car record stores a plate number, model, and daily price. Customer records store the renter's name, surname, and address. Rental records store the rental date, due date, return date, total price, and leasing terms, and connect the rented car with the customer who rents it. When a customer wants to rent a car, the system shows the cars that are currently available and sorts them by daily price. The system records each rental, calculates total rental revenue, lists overdue customers when the current date passes a due date and the car has not been returned, computes the average daily price of all cars in the store, and counts how many rentals each customer has made. The main office issues overdue notices for late returns, and every overdue notice keeps a notice number so the notice can be tracked for the target customer.
Function Requirement:
1. Identify the cars that are currently available in the store. A car is available when it is not tied to an active rental whose return date is still empty. Return the available cars in ascending order of daily price, or return an empty list when none are available.
2. Calculate the total revenue generated by all rental records stored in the store.
3. List the customers whose rentals are overdue. A rental is overdue when its return date is empty and the current date is later than the due date. Return an empty list when no overdue customers exist, or when the current date input is null.
4. Determine the average daily price of all cars stored in the store. Return 0.0 when the store has no car records.
5. Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the result as a customer-to-count mapping. Return an empty map when the store has no rental records for any customer.
```

Post-condition: this.customer points to the provided customer. this.noticeId holds a valid, unique identifier. The method returns this noticeId. System state is updated only with the association and ID assignment.

3. List the customers whose rentals are overdue. A rental is overdue when its return date is empty and the current date is later than the due date. Return an empty list when no overdue customers exist, or when the current date input is null.
4. Determine the average daily price of all cars stored in the store. Return 0.0 when the store has no car records.
5. Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the result as a customer-to-count mapping. Return an empty map when the store has no rental records for any customer.
```

# Operation Specifications
Store::calculateTotalRevenue
Summary: Computes the cumulative financial earnings generated by all rental transactions recorded in the store.
Algorithm: 
  1. Initialize totalRevenue = 0.0.
  2. For each rental in this.rentals:
     a. totalRevenue += rental.totalPrice
  3. Return totalRevenue.
Related Features: rentals, Rental.totalPrice
Related Classes: Rental
Input: None
Output: double: The sum of totalPrice fields across all rental records. Value range: ≥ 0.0.
Pre-condition: this.rentals list is initialized (may be empty). All Rental objects contain valid, non-null numeric totalPrice values.
Post-condition: Returns the exact mathematical sum of all rental totals. The internal state of Store and Rental objects remains completely unchanged.

Store::determineAverageDailyPrice
Summary: Calculates the arithmetic mean of the daily rental rates for all vehicles currently in the store's inventory.
Algorithm: 
  1. If this.cars.size() == 0, return 0.0.
  2. Initialize priceSum = 0.0.
  3. For each car in this.cars:
     a. priceSum += car.dailyPrice
  4. Calculate average = priceSum / this.cars.size().
  5. Return average.
Related Features: cars, Car.dailyPrice
Related Classes: Car
Input: None
Output: double: The average daily price across all cars. Value range: ≥ 0.0. Returns exactly 0.0 when the store has no car records.
Pre-condition: this.cars list is initialized. All Car.dailyPrice values are valid non-negative numbers.
Post-condition: Returns the computed average. The cars collection remains unchanged.

Store::countCarsRentedPerCustomer
Summary: Aggregates rental activity to produce a mapping that associates each customer with the number of cars they have rented.
Algorithm: 
  1. Initialize an empty EMap<Customer, Integer> named customerCounts.
  2. For each rental in this.rentals:
     a. Retrieve cust = rental.customer.
     b. If cust exists in customerCounts, increment its value by 1; otherwise, insert (cust, 1) into customerCounts.
  3. Return customerCounts.
Related Features: rentals, Rental.customer
Related Classes: Customer, Rental
Input: None
Output: EMap<Customer, Integer>: A mapping where keys are Customer objects and values are their respective rental counts. Returns an empty map if this.rentals is empty or contains no valid entries.
Pre-condition: this.rentals list is initialized. All Rental.customer references are valid and non-null.
Post-condition: Returns a map reflecting the exact frequency of rentals per customer. System state remains unmodified.

Store::findCustomersWithOverdueRentals
Summary: Identifies customers who have active rentals that have passed their contractual due dates without being returned.
Algorithm: 
  1. If currentDate is null, return an empty List<Customer>.
  2. Initialize an empty list overdueCustomers.
  3. For each rental in this.rentals:
     a. If rental.backDate is null AND currentDate > rental.dueDate:
        i. Add rental.customer to overdueCustomers (skip if already present to ensure uniqueness).
  4. Return overdueCustomers.
Related Features: rentals, Rental.backDate, Rental.dueDate, Rental.customer
Related Classes: Customer, Rental
Input: currentDate (EDate): The reference date representing "today" for evaluating overdue status.
Output: List<Customer>: A list of unique customers with overdue rentals. Returns an empty list if currentDate is null or no rentals meet the overdue criteria.
Pre-condition: currentDate is not null. this.rentals list is initialized. EDate supports standard chronological comparison operators.
Post-condition: Returns a list of qualifying customers. Internal rental data, dates, and store state remain untouched.

Store::identifyAvailableCars
Summary: Filters the store's vehicle inventory to isolate cars not currently leased, then sorts them by cost efficiency.
Algorithm: 
  1. Initialize a temporary set activeRentedCars to track vehicles currently on lease.
  2. For each rental in this.rentals:
     a. If rental.backDate is null, add rental.car to activeRentedCars.
  3. Initialize an empty list availableCars.
  4. For each car in this.cars:
     a. If car is not in activeRentedCars, add car to availableCars.
  5. Sort availableCars in ascending order based on car.dailyPrice.
  6. Return availableCars.
Related Features: cars, rentals, Car.dailyPrice, Rental.car, Rental.backDate
Related Classes: Car, Rental
Input: None
Output: List<Car>: A list of available vehicles sorted from lowest to highest daily price. Returns an empty list if all cars are rented or inventory is empty.
Pre-condition: this.cars and this.rentals lists are initialized. rental.backDate correctly reflects return status (null indicates the car is still out).
Post-condition: Returns a newly created, sorted list of available cars. Original cars and rentals collections remain unmodified.

You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

# Requirement
```text
Store assistants manage a rented-car gallery for each store. Each store keeps a store name together with the car records, rental records, and overdue-notice records that belong to that location. Every car record stores a plate number, model, and daily price. Customer records store the renter's name, surname, and address. Rental records store the rental date, due date, return date, total price, and leasing terms, and connect the rented car with the customer who rents it. When a customer wants to rent a car, the system shows the cars that are currently available and sorts them by daily price. The system records each rental, calculates total rental revenue, lists overdue customers when the current date passes a due date and the car has not been returned, computes the average daily price of all cars in the store, and counts how many rentals each customer has made. The main office issues overdue notices for late returns, and every overdue notice keeps a notice number so the notice can be tracked for the target customer.
Function Requirement:
1. Identify the cars that are currently available in the store. A car is available when it is not tied to an active rental whose return date is still empty. Return the available cars in ascending order of daily price, or return an empty list when none are available.
2. Calculate the total revenue generated by all rental records stored in the store.
3. List the customers whose rentals are overdue. A rental is overdue when its return date is empty and the current date is later than the due date. Return an empty list when no overdue customers exist, or when the current date input is null.
4. Determine the average daily price of all cars stored in the store. Return 0.0 when the store has no car records.
5. Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the result as a customer-to-count mapping. Return an empty map when the store has no rental records for any customer.
```

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

  public EList<Car> identifyAvailableCars()
  {
    Set<Car> activeRentedCars = new HashSet<>();
    for (Rental rental : getRentals()) {
      if (rental.getBackDate() == null) {
        activeRentedCars.add(rental.getCar());
      }
    }
    
    List<Car> availableCars = new ArrayList<>();
    for (Car car : getCars()) {
      if (!activeRentedCars.contains(car)) {

      }

    Collections.sort(availableCars, Comparator.comparingDouble(Car::getDailyPrice));
    
    return new BasicEList<>(availableCars);

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Store assistants manage a rented-car gallery for each store. Each store keeps a store name together with the car records, rental records, and overdue-notice records that belong to that location. Every car record stores a plate number, model, and daily price. Customer records store the renter's name, surname, and address. Rental records store the rental date, due date, return date, total price, and leasing terms, and connect the rented car with the customer who rents it. When a customer wants to rent a car, the system shows the cars that are currently available and sorts them by daily price. The system records each rental, calculates total rental revenue, lists overdue customers when the current date passes a due date and the car has not been returned, computes the average daily price of all cars in the store, and counts how many rentals each customer has made. The main office issues overdue notices for late returns, and every overdue notice keeps a notice number so the notice can be tracked for the target customer.
Function Requirement:
1. Identify the cars that are currently available in the store. A car is available when it is not tied to an active rental whose return date is still empty. Return the available cars in ascending order of daily price, or return an empty list when none are available.
2. Calculate the total revenue generated by all rental records stored in the store.
3. List the customers whose rentals are overdue. A rental is overdue when its return date is empty and the current date is later than the due date. Return an empty list when no overdue customers exist, or when the current date input is null.
4. Determine the average daily price of all cars stored in the store. Return 0.0 when the store has no car records.
5. Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the result as a customer-to-count mapping. Return an empty map when the store has no rental records for any customer.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title carrental Model

    dailyPrice : double
    model : String
    plate : String
}

}

    calculateTotalRevenue() : double
    countCarsRentedPerCustomer() : EMap
    determineAverageDailyPrice() : double
    findCustomersWithOverdueRentals(currentDate : EDate) : List<Customer>
    identifyAvailableCars() : List<Car>
}

Rental --> Car : car

Store *-- "*" Rental : rentals
@enduml

Every car record stores a plate number, model, and daily price. Customer records store the renter's name, surname, and address. # Operation Specifications
Store::identifyAvailableCars
Summary: Filters and returns all vehicles not currently bound to an active, unreturned rental, sorted by cost efficiency.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize a Set 'occupiedCarIds' to track unavailable vehicles.
  2. Iterate through the 'rentals' list.
  3. For each rental where 'backDate' is empty/null, add the associated 'Rental.car' identifier to 'occupiedCarIds'.
  4. Initialize an empty List 'availableCars'.
  5. Iterate through the 'cars' list.
  6. If a car's identifier is not in 'occupiedCarIds', add it to 'availableCars'.
  7. Sort 'availableCars' in ascending order based on 'Car.dailyPrice'.
  8. Return 'availableCars' (returns empty list if no cars qualify).
Related Features: cars, rentals, Rental.backDate, Rental.car, Car.dailyPrice
Related Classes: Car, Rental
Input: None
Output: List<Car> - Available vehicles sorted ascending by daily price. Returns empty list when inventory is fully occupied or empty.
Pre-condition: Both 'cars' and 'rentals' lists are initialized; Car objects support identity/equality checks.
Post-condition: Returns a correctly filtered and sorted list of available cars. Original collections are untouched.

Store::findCustomersWithOverdueRentals
Summary: Retrieves a list of customers whose active rentals have exceeded their due dates relative to a provided reference date.
Algorithm:
  1. If 'currentDate' is null, return an empty List<Customer>.
  2. Initialize an empty Set 'overdueCustomerSet' to prevent duplicates.
  3. Iterate through each element in the 'rentals' list.
  4. Check if 'Rental.backDate' (return date) is empty/null AND 'currentDate' > 'Rental.dueDate'.
  5. If both conditions are met, add 'Rental.customer' to 'overdueCustomerSet'.
  6. Convert the set to a List and return it.
Related Features: rentals, Rental.backDate, Rental.dueDate, Rental.customer
Related Classes: Customer, EDate
Input: currentDate (EDate) - The reference point for evaluating overdue status. Can be null.
Output: List<Customer> - Unique customers with overdue rentals. Returns an empty list if input is null or no overdue rentals exist.
Pre-condition: The 'rentals' list is initialized; EDate supports comparison operators (>); currentDate may be null.
Post-condition: Returns a deduplicated list of customers meeting the overdue criteria. Store data remains unmodified.

Store::countCarsRentedPerCustomer
Summary: Tally the number of rental records associated with each individual customer and returns the mapping.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize an empty hash map 'rentalCounts' of type <Customer, Integer>.
  2. Iterate through each element in the 'rentals' list.
  3. Extract the 'customer' reference from the current rental.
  4. Retrieve the existing count for the customer from 'rentalCounts'; default to 0 if absent.
  5. Increment the count by 1 and update the entry in 'rentalCounts'.
  6. After iteration, return 'rentalCounts'.
Related Features: rentals, Rental.customer
Related Classes: Customer, EMap
Input: None
Output: EMap - A mapping of Customer objects to their respective rental count (Integer). Returns an empty map if no rentals exist.
Pre-condition: The 'rentals' list is initialized; Customer objects implement proper equals/hashCode for map key resolution.
Post-condition: The returned map accurately reflects the frequency of rentals per customer. Original store state remains unchanged.

Store::calculateTotalRevenue
Summary: Computes the cumulative financial gain generated by all rental transactions recorded in the store.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize accumulator variable 'totalRevenue' to 0.0.
  2. Iterate through each element in the 'rentals' list.
  3. For each rental record, add its 'totalPrice' value to 'totalRevenue'.
  4. After iteration completes, return 'totalRevenue'.
Related Features: rentals, Rental.totalPrice
Related Classes: Rental
Input: None
Output: double - The sum of all rental total prices. Value range: >= 0.0. Returns 0.0 if the rentals list is empty.
Pre-condition: The 'rentals' list is initialized (may be empty); all Rental.totalPrice values are valid numeric types.
Post-condition: Returns the exact arithmetic sum of rental revenues without modifying any underlying data structures.

Store::determineAverageDailyPrice
Summary: Calculates the arithmetic mean of the daily rental rates for all vehicles registered in the store.
Algorithm:
  1. Check if the 'cars' list is empty or null. If true, return 0.0 immediately.
  2. Initialize 'priceSum' to 0.0 and 'count' to 0.
  3. Iterate through each element in the 'cars' list.
  4. Accumulate 'Car.dailyPrice' into 'priceSum' and increment 'count'.
  5. Compute average = 'priceSum' / 'count'.
  6. Return the computed average.
Related Features: cars, Car.dailyPrice
Related Classes: Car
Input: None
Output: double - The average daily price across all cars. Value range: >= 0.0. Returns 0.0 when no car records exist.
Pre-condition: The 'cars' list is initialized; all Car.dailyPrice values are non-negative numbers.
Post-condition: Returns the precise mean of vehicle daily rates. No modifications to the cars collection occur.

/*

 */

class_1::operation_2
/*
 * revised description for this operation_2

2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
Store assistants manage a rented-car gallery for each store. Each store keeps a store name together with the car records, rental records, and overdue-notice records that belong to that location. Every car record stores a plate number, model, and daily price. Customer records store the renter's name, surname, and address. Rental records store the rental date, due date, return date, total price, and leasing terms, and connect the rented car with the customer who rents it. When a customer wants to rent a car, the system shows the cars that are currently available and sorts them by daily price. The system records each rental, calculates total rental revenue, lists overdue customers when the current date passes a due date and the car has not been returned, computes the average daily price of all cars in the store, and counts how many rentals each customer has made. The main office issues overdue notices for late returns, and every overdue notice keeps a notice number so the notice can be tracked for the target customer.
Function Requirement:
1. Identify the cars that are currently available in the store. A car is available when it is not tied to an active rental whose return date is still empty. Return the available cars in ascending order of daily price, or return an empty list when none are available.
2. Calculate the total revenue generated by all rental records stored in the store.
3. List the customers whose rentals are overdue. A rental is overdue when its return date is empty and the current date is later than the due date. Return an empty list when no overdue customers exist, or when the current date input is null.
4. Determine the average daily price of all cars stored in the store. Return 0.0 when the store has no car records.
5. Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the result as a customer-to-count mapping. Return an empty map when the store has no rental records for any customer.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title carrental Model

class Car {
    dailyPrice : double
    model : String
    plate : String
}
class Customer {
    address : String
    name : String
    surname : String
}
class OverdueNotice {
    noticeId : String
    customer : Customer
    sendNoticeTo(customer : Customer) : String
}
class Rental {
    backDate : EDate
    dueDate : EDate
    leasingTerms : String
    rentalDate : EDate
    totalPrice : double
    car : Car
    customer : Customer
}
class Store {
    name : String
    cars : List<Car>
    notices : List<OverdueNotice>
    rentals : List<Rental>
    calculateTotalRevenue() : double
    countCarsRentedPerCustomer() : EMap
    determineAverageDailyPrice() : double
    findCustomersWithOverdueRentals(currentDate : EDate) : List<Customer>
    identifyAvailableCars() : List<Car>
}

OverdueNotice --> Customer : customer
Rental --> Car : car
Rental --> Customer : customer
Store *-- "*" Car : cars
Store *-- "*" OverdueNotice : notices
Store *-- "*" Rental : rentals
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
Store::identifyAvailableCars
Summary: Filters and returns all vehicles not currently bound to an active, unreturned rental, sorted by cost efficiency.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize a Set 'occupiedCarIds' to track unavailable vehicles.
  2. Iterate through the 'rentals' list.
  3. For each rental where 'backDate' is empty/null, add the associated 'Rental.car' identifier to 'occupiedCarIds'.
  4. Initialize an empty List 'availableCars'.
  5. Iterate through the 'cars' list.
  6. If a car's identifier is not in 'occupiedCarIds', add it to 'availableCars'.
  7. Sort 'availableCars' in ascending order based on 'Car.dailyPrice'.
  8. Return 'availableCars' (returns empty list if no cars qualify).
Related Features: cars, rentals, Rental.backDate, Rental.car, Car.dailyPrice
Related Classes: Car, Rental
Input: None
Output: List<Car> - Available vehicles sorted ascending by daily price. Returns empty list when inventory is fully occupied or empty.
Pre-condition: Both 'cars' and 'rentals' lists are initialized; Car objects support identity/equality checks.
Post-condition: Returns a correctly filtered and sorted list of available cars. Original collections are untouched.

Store::findCustomersWithOverdueRentals
Summary: Retrieves a list of customers whose active rentals have exceeded their due dates relative to a provided reference date.
Algorithm:
  1. If 'currentDate' is null, return an empty List<Customer>.
  2. Initialize an empty Set 'overdueCustomerSet' to prevent duplicates.
  3. Iterate through each element in the 'rentals' list.
  4. Check if 'Rental.backDate' (return date) is empty/null AND 'currentDate' > 'Rental.dueDate'.
  5. If both conditions are met, add 'Rental.customer' to 'overdueCustomerSet'.
  6. Convert the set to a List and return it.
Related Features: rentals, Rental.backDate, Rental.dueDate, Rental.customer
Related Classes: Customer, EDate
Input: currentDate (EDate) - The reference point for evaluating overdue status. Can be null.
Output: List<Customer> - Unique customers with overdue rentals. Returns an empty list if input is null or no overdue rentals exist.
Pre-condition: The 'rentals' list is initialized; EDate supports comparison operators (>); currentDate may be null.
Post-condition: Returns a deduplicated list of customers meeting the overdue criteria. Store data remains unmodified.

Store::countCarsRentedPerCustomer
Summary: Tally the number of rental records associated with each individual customer and returns the mapping.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize an empty hash map 'rentalCounts' of type <Customer, Integer>.
  2. Iterate through each element in the 'rentals' list.
  3. Extract the 'customer' reference from the current rental.
  4. Retrieve the existing count for the customer from 'rentalCounts'; default to 0 if absent.
  5. Increment the count by 1 and update the entry in 'rentalCounts'.
  6. After iteration, return 'rentalCounts'.
Related Features: rentals, Rental.customer
Related Classes: Customer, EMap
Input: None
Output: EMap - A mapping of Customer objects to their respective rental count (Integer). Returns an empty map if no rentals exist.
Pre-condition: The 'rentals' list is initialized; Customer objects implement proper equals/hashCode for map key resolution.
Post-condition: The returned map accurately reflects the frequency of rentals per customer. Original store state remains unchanged.

Store::calculateTotalRevenue
Summary: Computes the cumulative financial gain generated by all rental transactions recorded in the store.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize accumulator variable 'totalRevenue' to 0.0.
  2. Iterate through each element in the 'rentals' list.
  3. For each rental record, add its 'totalPrice' value to 'totalRevenue'.
  4. After iteration completes, return 'totalRevenue'.
Related Features: rentals, Rental.totalPrice
Related Classes: Rental
Input: None
Output: double - The sum of all rental total prices. Value range: >= 0.0. Returns 0.0 if the rentals list is empty.
Pre-condition: The 'rentals' list is initialized (may be empty); all Rental.totalPrice values are valid numeric types.
Post-condition: Returns the exact arithmetic sum of rental revenues without modifying any underlying data structures.

Store::determineAverageDailyPrice
Summary: Calculates the arithmetic mean of the daily rental rates for all vehicles registered in the store.
Algorithm:
  1. Check if the 'cars' list is empty or null. If true, return 0.0 immediately.
  2. Initialize 'priceSum' to 0.0 and 'count' to 0.
  3. Iterate through each element in the 'cars' list.
  4. Accumulate 'Car.dailyPrice' into 'priceSum' and increment 'count'.
  5. Compute average = 'priceSum' / 'count'.
  6. Return the computed average.
Related Features: cars, Car.dailyPrice
Related Classes: Car
Input: None
Output: double - The average daily price across all cars. Value range: >= 0.0. Returns 0.0 when no car records exist.
Pre-condition: The 'cars' list is initialized; all Car.dailyPrice values are non-negative numbers.
Post-condition: Returns the precise mean of vehicle daily rates. No modifications to the cars collection occur.

# Operation Specifications
OverdueNotice::sendNoticeTo
Summary: Generates and dispatches an overdue notice to a specified customer, assigning a unique tracking identifier (notice ID) to the notice instance.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that the input 'customer' parameter is not null.
  2. Generate a unique notice identifier (e.g., combining a store prefix, timestamp, and sequence number).
  3. Assign the generated identifier to the 'noticeId' attribute.
  4. Associate the target customer with the 'customer' attribute.
  5. Format and return the notice ID as a string representation for tracking purposes.
Related Features: noticeId, customer
Related Classes: Customer
Input: customer (Customer) - The target renter receiving the overdue notice. Must not be null.
Output: String - The generated unique notice ID used for tracking and reference. Non-empty string format.
Pre-condition: The OverdueNotice instance is instantiated; customer parameter is valid and not null.
Post-condition: The 'noticeId' attribute holds a unique, non-null identifier; the 'customer' attribute references the input customer; the returned string matches the assigned notice ID.

   * @generated NOT

  public EList<Car> identifyAvailableCars()
  {
    Set<Car> occupiedCarRefs = new HashSet<>();
    for (Rental rental : getRentals()) {
        if (rental.getBackDate() == null) {
            occupiedCarRefs.add(rental.getCar());
        }
    }
    
    List<Car> availableCars = new ArrayList<>();
    for (Car car : getCars()) {
        if (!occupiedCarRefs.contains(car)) {
            availableCars.add(car);

    return new BasicEList<>(availableCars);
  }

  public EList<Customer> findCustomersWithOverdueRentals(final Date currentDate)
  {
    if (currentDate == null) {
        return new BasicEList<>();
    }
    
    Set<Customer> overdueCustomerSet = new HashSet<>();
    for (Rental rental : getRentals()) {
        if (rental.getBackDate() == null && currentDate.after(rental.getDueDate())) {
            overdueCustomerSet.add(rental.getCustomer());
        }
    }
    
    return new BasicEList<>(new ArrayList<>(overdueCustomerSet));
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Calculates the arithmetic mean of the daily rental rates for all vehicles registered in the store.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Check if the 'cars' list is empty or null. If true, return 0.0 immediately.
   *   2. Initialize 'priceSum' to 0.0 and 'count' to 0.
   *   3. Iterate through each element in the 'cars' list.
   *   4. Accumulate 'Car.dailyPrice' into 'priceSum' and increment 'count'.
   *   5. Compute average = 'priceSum' / 'count'.
   *   6. Return the computed average.
   * Related Features: cars, Car.dailyPrice

   * Input: None

   * Pre-condition: The 'cars' list is initialized; all Car.dailyPrice values are non-negative numbers.
   * Post-condition: Returns the precise mean of vehicle daily rates. No modifications to the cars collection occur.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public double determineAverageDailyPrice()
  {
    if (getCars().isEmpty()) {
        return 0.0;
    }
    
    double priceSum = 0.0;
    int count = 0;
    for (Car car : getCars()) {
        priceSum += car.getDailyPrice();
        count++;
    }
    
    return priceSum / count;
  }

  public Map<Customer, Integer> countCarsRentedPerCustomer()
  {

        Customer customer = rental.getCustomer();
        Integer count = rentalCounts.getOrDefault(customer, 0);
        rentalCounts.put(customer, count + 1);
    }
    return rentalCounts;
  }
